January 1, 1993


To Sacred Harp Singers and Friends:
This is your new record of Sacred Harp Singing held during 1992 We trust
that the record will be of interest to you. Use it to find a singing to attend
when you wish to go to a Sacred Harp Singing in 1993.


Ask yourself this question: Am I going to the singing to sing praises to the
Lord and glorify his name or, am l going to criticize some one, find fault
and make hurting remarks about some one? Sacred Harp Music is a form of
worship, let's go with a song in our heart and a prayer for our fellow
singers and praises for our Lord. If we do this, we will see our singing
begin to improve.
We had only five singings called off this year; this is a big improvement
over last year. But in the past nine years we have had seventy one singings
discontinued. We have some new ones added this year all from the
Northern states. Lets give them a big hand for doing a good job.
Please send your minutes in on time. Price for printing is $ 25.00 per day
singing, and $ 12.50 per night singing. Make your check to Sacred Harp
Singings.
The minutes for the month of December 1992 will be printed in the front of
your 1993 minute book, This is done, so you will receive your minutes in
February of 1993.
Hope each of you have a good singing year.


Nora Parker, Secretary

OLD DEKALB COUNTY COURTHOUSE IN DECATUR
Decatur Georgia
Saturday, September 21, 1991
A special singing convention was hold in the courtroom of the old
Dekalb County Courthouse on the square in Decatur, Georgia, on September
21. The event was held to remember the B. F White Memorial singings
that were held in that room from 1897 until the mid-1960s. Doug Allison
led "Return Again" on page 335 and E. C. Bowen led the morning prayer.

The Memorial lesson began with Ruth Brown asking the class to close their
eys and try to recall the most beautiful thing they had ever seen. She
brought to mind some of the things remembered, such as a beautiful
Rainbow, a colorful flower, or a special sunrise with its promise of a fresh
new start. Probably one of the most beautiful things in the world is a true
loyal trusting friend. The names on our list today were such friends. She
brought to mind several beautiful saying about friends.
Death tor these people could be compared to a sunset, a peaceful end to a
happy lite or released from the misery of pain and unhappiness. happiness
seems made to be shared, so does our grief. We so share with families and
friends the grief they felt in losing loved ones. We pause to remember them
with this memorial. The deceased from Alabama were: Leonard Kerr,
Graves Woodham, Milburn O. Kelley, Louis Chesser, Lovella Warren,
Gladys Kerr, Walter Parker, Berta Tant, John Moore, Merle Mixon, Jethro
Collette, Bertha Parker, Cecil Parker, and Jewell Deason. Lora Cargo led
song on page 285 in their memory.
The Georgia deceased were: E. G.Akin, J. C. Hughes, H. E. Denney.
Lonnie Rogers led page 283 in their memory. Deceased from Florida and
Texas, Fennie Clark, Jewell Owens, Danile Owens. and Bea Powell.
Victoria Aplin led 395b; For the sick and shut -ins Brenda Chafin led 31.
The memorial was closed with prayer by Ruth Brown. Regular singing
resumed with Alice Mosely, 572; Winston Jones, 225; Arvin Nall, 558;
Earl Nall, 127; Alice Edwards, 104; Lovella Kerr, 435; B. J. Harris, 107;

FOUR - NOTE SINGING
Marion, Kentucky
January 25, 1992
William (Bill) Paris began the morning by welcoming singers and quests
to the second annulal singing using two different four note books. He acted
as chairmen for the day, Tim Reynolds served as vice chairman, and Karen
Isbell Secretary. Bill Paris announced that the morning would contain one
hour's singing from The Southern Harmony, followed by one hour from The
Original Sacred Harp. After lunch, another hour of singing would occur
from each book. Tim Reynolds distributed a list of 150 songs which are
common to both books.
The chairman then called to order the first hour of singing by
demonstrating the Pendulum for keeping time as explained in the
introduction to The Southern Harmony. Knots in the string cause the
pendulum to sway a different speeds, setting the time for 2/4, 4/4, 6/8
and so on. Mr Paris led songs on pages 103 and 259 from Southern
Harmony. Leaders: Tim reynolds, 290, 291; Don Waggener, 296, 305;
David Killingsworth, 46, 38; Arnold Hunt, 71,89b; Bill Paris, 90; Don
Bardsley, 213; Bill Paris, 210.
RECESS.
Tim Reynolds called the class together leading song on page 84. Leaders
Karen Isbell, 61, 36b; Kathleen Thro, 108, 171; Kathy Hull, 85, 143;
Mary Ruth Cannon, 133, 108b; Nancy Paris, 47b, 159; Don Waggener,
455; Don Bardsley, 58.
DISMISSED FOR LUNCH.
The afternoon session was called to order by William Paris with a roll
call by state. About 40 singers were present from Kentucky, Tennessee,
Missouri and Illinois. From the Southern Harmony, he led songs on pages
11 and 192b; David Killingsworth, 45, 324; Don Waggener, 322, 10; Tim
Reynonds, 84, 15; Bill Parks, 39, 292; Don Bardsley, 306, 189. After
announcements, the following leaders led from the Sacred Harp: Tim
Reynolds, 64, 200; Karen Isbell, 441, 440; Kathleen Thro, 378b, 542;
Kathy Hull, 551: Mari Ruth Cannon, 153; Don Waggener, 338; Bill Paris,
312b; Don Bardsley, 300, 28; David Killingsworth, 40; Bill Paris, 89.

SUNDAY, APRIL 5, 1992
The Sunday session of the convention met at the Great Falls Grange in
Great Falls, Virginia. The morning session was called to order by Steven
Sabol leading song on page 36b. The morning prayer was led by John delRe.
Leaders: Tim Slattery, 1192; Rhonda Hendershot, 198; John Seaton 504;
Brian Sears, 48b: Claire Chapin, 84; Mary Helen Shortridge, 163b;
Howard Denson Brady, 274; Bob Donaldson, 147; Sue Hanson, 38; Carol
Werba (NY), 569b; Judy Mincey, 77b; Stephen McMaster 161; Mary K
Friday, 209; Ann O"Brien, 456; Joanne DeVoe, 287; Bill Holt, 70b.
RECESS.
The class reconvened with Peter Pate leading song on page 37b.
Leaders: Mary Hulbert, 129, Joe Bessley, 358; Connie Karduck, 494;
Deane Root, (PA), 155; Lisa Grayson, 444; Tom Padwa, 56b; Denise
Morris, 184; Doris Dyen (PA) 79; Geroge Seiler 186; Jennifer Cook,
300; Eric Bean, 181. The memorial lesson was held at this time. Amanda
Denson, assisted by Amanda Burdette Brady, made the following remarks:
"For many of us, every Sacred Harp Singing is a memorial. Evertime I
walk into a Sacred Harp singing, it is like l am five gears old. There is so
much security and love here, and I derive so much strength from the
traditions, the music, old and new friends, family members present, and
the memory of other family and friends. Those who have gone on before us
are singing in a class in which we will all be grateful and happy to be
singing in tenth row. Every name that will be called today was close to
someone in this room, and we can all remember our own special
relationships that we have lost during our lifetime. As long as we all live
and remember them, they will never die, for they live in our hearts. A
person's life is not measured by possessions but by who and what one is
and what one has accomplished in the time God has allotted him or her It is
not the length but the depth of the life that counts. We hear a lot of
sermons, but we don't get to see many. Some l've seen that have moved me
most have been in Sacred Harp singers' lives, past and present. God has
made the " Valley of the shadow" as beautiful as the daybreak. All of the
leaves turn beautiful colors, and then they fall to the ground, and then they
die, "and we all do fade as a leaf" (lsaiah 64:6). Our own day will come."
Then Hugh McGraw and Amanda Denson led 320 for all deceased of times
past. Pat Hilgard read the names of the recently deceased and sick. Jim
Carnes led 218 for Stven Anthony, who is sick and shut-in. Ted Mercer led
348 for the deceased in the Midwest. Michael Passwaters, Paul Henderson,


51


John Beabout, Paul Caddisy, Martin Rubenstein, and Irene Smith. Eric Bean
and George Seiler led 122 for the deceased fo the Northeast: Francesca
Cioiotti, Virginia Smith, David Kooh, John B. McCarty, Joan Esk, S. Donnis
and Christine Thun (Puerto Rico). Keith Willard led 267 for the deceased of
the Mid-Atlantic Area and West Coast: Blanche Wilson, Shirley Oosting,
Carol Chesney, Jalmer Anderson, Margaret Clark, and Lois Willard. Buell

LOUISIANA STATE SACRED HARP CONVENTION
New Orleans Baptist Theological Seminary, May 1-2, 1992
3939 Gentilly Blvd. New Orleans, Louisiana
Following an soup and Salad supper in the cafeteria building on the
NOBTS campus, the third annual Louisiana State Sacred Harp Convention
was called to order on May 1 at 7:15 p. m. with a welcome to the 37
persons present by Dr. Harry Eskew. Also, he extended a special welcome
to George and Cecile Cox from Tuscaloosa, Alabama. They were kind
enough to bring several copies of the new Sacred Harp book for purchase
and use during the singing. Persons from three states were present. Dr.
Eskew, president of the singing led song on page 49. Election of officers
was held with Dr. Eskew presiding. Dr. Eskew explained that of last years
officers he was the only one who could continue as an officer for the
coming year if the convention chose him again. He was elected by
acolamation as Chairman. Vice Chairman, Nancy Van Den Akker;
Secretaries, Ken Gabrielse and Chris Jenkins. Leaders: Nanci Van Den
Akker, 44; James Fields, 59: Daniel McNeil, 45; George Cox, 490; Ken
Gabrielese, 40; Dr. Eskew 47; Don Smith, 159; Elizabeth Muhleisen, 68b;
Virginia Fields, 452b; Danny Rester, 63; James Fields, 85; Rick McLain,
52; Dr. Eskew closed the singing with prayer.
SATURDAY
On May 2, the singing began at 9: 05 a.m. with informal singing of new
songs and fellowship around the table. at 9:45 a.m. The singing was
officially called to order by Dr. Eskew led 49b and led in prayer to open the
morning session. Leaders: Dr. Martha Hicks, 145; Nancy Van Den Akker,
84; Cecile Cox 135; Barry Aymond, 72b; Mike McNeil, 384; Ken
Gabrielse, 99; Jean Gray, 108; Rindy McNeil, 145b; Nella Brainis, 73b;
Adonna Lucas, 260; Mike Parrish, 155; Emily Smart, 267; Durward
Scarborogh, 339; Dr. Leroy Yarbrough, 147; Jay Hellums, 146; Nella
64


Brainis, 128; Nancy Van Den Akker 480; Dr. Harry Eskew, 117; George
 
 
Cox, 229; Mike McNeil, 101.
RECESS.
Dr. Eskew made some announcements and the possibility of changing the
State Singing to a fall date was discussed. A straw vote was taken with the
majority of the 32 present voting to have a fall singing. An offering was
taken for inclusion of these minutes in the National Minutes and for
publicity for the next State singing. Leaders: Ken Gabrielse, 371; Rick
McLain, 39; Adonna Lucas, 35; Cecile Cox, 274.

Evelyn Harris (AL), 378; Leyland delRe (VA), 28; Sue Kesssel, 442; Alice

(AL), 421; Dewey Williams (AL), 36b. B. M. Smith reported for the
Finance Committee. Lisa Grayson gave the report of the Resolutions
Committee: We thank our Creator for all blessings bestowed on us. As the
Memorial Committee so eloquentley stated, all of us are continuing a
traditon perpetuated by generations of singers who loved this music. We
wish to acknowledge our debt and gratitude to everyone who has kept the
Sacred Harp traditon alive, including many of the singers in this room.
Thanks to all visitors, whether they traveled a few blocks or a few
hundred miles to sing with us. We are especially grateful to our wiregrass
friends, whose music has left all of us asking for just a little more time.
Thanks to Ruth Brown, who organized the Alabama bus trip even while
arranging for the funeral and burial of her husband last week. Thanks to
John and Janice Johnson, who helped arrange the trip for our Wiregrass
guests. We are grateful to the locating committee for finding these
wonderful rooms to sing in; to the University of Chicago Folklore Society;
to the officers of this year's and last year's convention; and to the
arranging committee, whose deft efforts made for two wonderful days of
singing. Thank you, Ted and Marcia Johnson, for a lovely social evening.
We'd like to express our appreciation and gratitude to all who prepared
food for dinner both days. Special thanks to the unsung heroine of the
weekend, Janice Collins, whose miracles of organnization and calm in the
eye of the kitchen hurricane have made our dinners possible for years of
conventions. Thanks to everyone who helped work behind the scenes.
There is one other person we would like to recongnize. His name does not
appear on the the list of officers nor is he a member of any committee, so
there is no written record of all he had done. But his vision, his endless
patience and seemingley endless labor and his unwavering grace under
pressure made this convention possible, Mr Ted Mercer.( At this point the
class gave Ted a standing ovation.) Connie Karduck, Judy Hauff and Jerry
Enright led the closing song on page 62 and all who wished took the parting
hand. The convention was dismissed with prayer bu B. J. Harris.

Enright.

RECESS.
The class was reassembled with Mark Davis lesding song on page 91.
Leaders: Daphene Causey, 302; Walter Wade (TN), 85; Erskin
Vandegrift, Jr., 59; B. J. Harris, 452; Joan Aldridge, 344; Myrtis
Graham, 551; Harry Eskew (LA), 146; Evelyn Harris, 47; Hugh McGraw,
352; Richard DeLong, 472. A memorial lesson was held. Jesse Roberts
read the following meditation: The human condition is that humans die. We
must face this more surely than any other common human trait. Death of
loved ones may be the greatest test of our faith. During this time, we
must turn our eyes to God for a special reasure of faith. Singing helps us
do that.
Those singers for whom we will sing are now in heaven. Those whose
voices grew hoarse and weak now can sing praises to Christ eternally. As
we remember them and feel our loss, we must also rejoice that they are in
the presence of God.
It is also appropriate for us to remember out mentors, ta give them the
roses while they live. You know who your are. Some of mine have passed
away. Others, who took me under their wing and taught me how to sing
Sacred Harp, and who encouraged me to get more involved and to attend
conventions where l have met and learned to love all of you, deserve my
undying love, respect and gratitude.
We should also be an example to the next gereration as we go through
these memorial lessons. They are a bridge from mentor to apprentice,
from the oldest to the youngest generation. We must teach our children, by
our example, to respect the tradition and revere those who taught us this
tradition.
Mark Davis read the names of those who have passed on. While we
sing, let us meditate on the joy they gave us and strengthen our resolve to
pass this joy on to the next generstion. The deceased form Alabara were:
Leman Brown, J. H. Ballinger, Luther Parker, Maxine Aaron, C.W. Baines,
J. Chester Creel, Etta Bell Blackwood, Oscar Jenkins, Jethro Collett,
Virgil Brothers, Maud Doss Harris, J. C. Deason, Jewell Deason Agnes
Burns, Buell Cobb, Sr., Robert Spivey. Toney Smith led 477 for the
Alabama decessed. The decessed for other states were: Groegia: Hoyt
Cagle, Carl Hughes, Elder W.S. Kelly, Bill Hendrix; Mississippi: Sadie Van
Horn, Robert Everett, Sam Mayes, Sara Delaria; Texas, Helene Beard,
Wesley Godsey; Ohio Deborah Schober; New York: Catherine Vawter;
Illinoise: John Beabout. Richard Delong led 163b for the deceased from
other states. Ralph Heath led 376 for the sick and shut-ins. Mark David
closed the memorial lesson with prayer. Leaders: Beth Hoffman (IL), 89;
Ruby Philips, 290; Kelly Bard, 378.

and Hilda Morrison, 332; Eloise Watkins, 454. The convention went into a
business session and recived the following reports: Resolutions Committee
The Resolutions Commiteee would first like to give thanks to God for
allowing us the opportunity to meet together once again. We gratefull
acknowledge the generosity of Samford University in providing a facility
where we can meet to sing. A special word of appreciation goes to those
who helped to prepare food each day so that we could sustain ourselves for
another day of singing. We acknowledge the work of all the officers and
committees, for without them the convention would flounder. We express
appreciation to all of the singers and listeners who came from near and far
to raise their voices in song as one, to make this convention a success.
Most importantly, we recognize the love and fellowship which exists
among our singing family. Repectfully submitted Elene Stovall, Joan
Aldridge. Finance Commitee: Jim Carnes reported that $1079.00 had been
raised, which would cover convention expenses with a little seed monay
left over. Secretary's report: over the past three days we have sung 230
songs led by 169 different leaders from 19 states: Alabama, 83; Georgia,
22: Massachusetts, 13; Texas, 7; Mississippi and Illinois, 6 each;
Virginia, 4; Oklahoma, North Carolina and Tennessee, 3 each; Ohio, Ney
York, and Minnesota, 2 each; Colorado, California, Rhode Island, Missouri
and Louisiana, 1 each. There were 275 registrants from 20 states, the 19
listed above, plus Washington State. The reports of the various committees
were adopted. Susan Mampre proposed an evening singing session to sing
works composed since the 1991 Revision, to be titled The Hugh HcGraw
Living Tradition Session. Discussion of this proposal followed, and the
business session was closed. Announcements of upcoming singings were
made, and the four officers plus the Chairman Emeritus led song on page 62
as the closing song, while all who wished took the parting hand. Some 65
singers proceeded to Lynn Park in downtown Birmingham, where they
presented a 45 minute program as part of the Alabama Folklife Festival's
City Stages.
Chairman. Virgil Phillips Vice Chairman, Warren Steel: Secretary, Paula
McGray.

Resolutions Committee: The 1992 Resolutions Committee report is in the
121


form of a prayer. So let's go to the Lord in prayer: "Almighty God, we
remember the thankful praises of your servants Solomon when he wrote in
the Seventy-first Psalm these words: I will praise you with the harp for
your faithfulness, O my God; I will sing praise to you with the lyre, O Holy
one of lsrael. My lips will shout for joy when I sing praises to you: l, whom
you have redeemed, and my tongue will tell of your richteous acts all day
long. (Psalm 71:22-24). "Lord God, Heavenly and most merciful father, we
have gathered here in this sacred spot for the last two days to sing,
because your are an awesom God and truly worthy of our praise. We
humbley pray, Lord, that our meager songs have been pleasing to your ear
and that our gifts of love have been worthy in your sight. We thank you,
Lord, for this time of fellowship and worship here together, for without
your love and the power of your Holy Sprit, it would not have been
possible.
Oh Gracious Father, we come before you asking nothing for ourselves,
but we would ask for your blessings to be poured out on the Wilson family
for welcoming us once again into their sacred bower of prayer. Lord, there
is no way we can know of, or even begin to repay, your tireless servants
for their hours of preparation. Only you know all their names; only you
know all their selfless deeds. Lord, we ask that you pour out your special
blessings on each and every one of these, your saints.
And Father we also ask your blessings for the ones who served by
keeping your commandments: feeding the hungry, caring for the widows
and ophans, welcoming the stranger and working to lead your flock.
gracious God, it is not by our own power that we come before you asking
these things, but only because your Holy Son, Jesus Christ, taught us to be
so bold, and it is in His beautiful name we ask all these things. Amen,
Respectfully submitted, Richard Whatley, Resolutions Committee.
Karen Rollis, gave the finance report. Since there were no futher
business the session closed after adoption of the reports. Sheir Taylor led
294 and announcements were made. The convention closed in the traditional
manner with the chairman and Vice chairman leading song on page 62. The
Chaplain, L. L. Rogers dismissed with prager
Chairman, Sheir Taylor; Vice Chairman, David McGukin; Secretary, Karen
Rollins; Assistant Secretary, Vivian Rogers.

time. Today, we hold in precious memory: Rev. Roger Neil Deschner,
Helene Beard, J. H. Ballinger, Lillie Stokers, Elder Virgil Stanaland, Allye
Paine Murphy, James Henry Rousseau, Robert Davis, Robert Spivey,
Albert Watkins, Winnie Johnston, Blanchie Bridges, Chester Creel, Esther
Holland and Elton Martindale. In the seventh chapter of Ecclestiastes we
read a strange and puzzling thought: A good name is better than precious
ointment; and the day of death than the day of one's birth. For the christian
this has to be true. In Revelations 2:2 we are reminded; I know thy works
and thy labor and thy patience and in verse 3. How for my name's sake
thou hath laboured and not fainted. Joy is a product of Salvation. Joy on
earth becomes jubilation in heaven. The redeerned sing a song that the
angels cannot sing. Isaiah 12, speaks of the joy of salvation v 3: With joy
you will draw water from the wells of salvation. Luke 5, in the return of
the prodigal son, speaks of the joy of repentance, and in the first chapter
of James we learn that those who presevere under trial and stand the test
will receive the crown of life. Our departed friends rejoyce in that their
faith is now made sight. As always, the message today is for those who
remain. In Ecclesiastes 12:13, we are admonished: Let us hear the whole
conclusion of the matter : Fear God and keep his cornmandments, for this is
the whole duty of Man, And Ecclestiastes 12:5,6 tells us For man goeth to
his long home, and mourners go about the streets; or even the silver cord
be loosed or the golden bowl broken, or the pitcher be broken at the
fountain, or the wheel be broken at the cistern. Then shall the dust return
to the earth as it was; and the spirit shall return to God who gave it. While
we may feel somewhat decimated by the home-going of so many, we are
reminded in another precious scripture that Joy cometh in the morning. A
gospel song speaks of this Joy. For out benediction, Psalm 90:17, And let
the beauty of the Lord Our God be upon us; and establish thou the work of
our hands upon us; Yea, the work of our hands establish thou it. Aman!
Sam Craig read the names of the sick and shut-ins: Leonard and Maxine

Keith Willard brought the afternoon session together leading song on
page 203. Leaders: Jerry Enright and Carol Buche, 77; Steven Parker
299, 31; Melanie Hauff, 34, 444; Mark Ellenberger, 38, 455; Jeanette
Lowry, 454, 39; Robin Fox, 135, 315; Sandy Hill, 43, 99. A memorial
lesson was led by Steven Parker in memory of his grandmother. He made
the following remarks: "It is good that we have a gospel trumpet to alert
those who we are about to honor. And now we have come to the part of our
convention where we remember those who have passed from our presence
in the preceding year or who are too sick to join their voices with us. It is
a time when we acknowledge how community requires a brokern Heart. My
grandmother died a month ago today, the friend and spiritual companion of
my early life whose curiosity and compassion gave me the heart as a
psychologist to do the hard work of psychotherapy. (She always thought
I'd be a preacher.) We sat in the early mornings when I was young, before
the others had risen, breakfasing on strawberries and ice cream and talk
ing about everything in the world Every person who has ever found healing
in a relationship with me has been touched by her love. It is in this way, I
think, that the divine mother reaches into the world through us.
And so stand before you today with a heart broken open. And I think
we need to have our hearts broken from time to time. A closed heart,
thouth it may seem, at first, safe and secure, is a trap. And we all know
something of how our greatest skills in this world grow from our very
167


brokenness. How these openings in who we thought we were become the
way to connect with what is other and greater. By her death and by many
other events this year l am broken open- and l know that to the extent that
I try to shore up my safety and security l get trapped. And so I pray for
courage to bear a broken heart and wisdom to see the light streaming
through the openings. I know that although a physical change in our
friendship has removed her physical presence, she still touches me in my
heart each time my mind remembers her, as I am certain you are
remembering and being touched this minute by others on this list. In the
moment of this memorial lesson we close the circle of community with
these removed, and remember with each other our brokenness as we sing
our hearts out to them. The decease are" John O'Heara Lexau, Rache Park
Gifford, William Holland, George White, Jalmer "Andy" Anderson, Robert N.
Hoxie, JOhn Beabout, Shirley Oosting, Helene Beard, Herb Schroeder's
father, Hershel Augsberger, Taylor Brown. The sick and shut-ins: Marvin
Anthony, Marjorie Parker, Karen Hojnacki, Caroline Inscoe. Steven
Parker then led song on page 122 in memory of the decease, he then read a
poem for the sick and shut-ins and led song on page 566. Regular singing
resumed with the following leaders: John Gurtz and Colette Miller, 500,
524; David Hough, 143, 290; Beverly Enright, 272, 245; Keith Willard,
497; Jim Parsons, 159, 383; Midge and Gordon Olsen, 269, 63; Christine
Stevens, 327, 155; Carol Enseki, 84; Bob Hoxie, 147 186; MaryAnn
Evans, 137; John Rebischke, 38b, 495; Graham Wood, 193, 217; Eleanor
Haase, 117; Gordon Olsen, 124, 86. The class was dismised with prayer
by Bob Hoxie.
Chairman, Steven Parker; Vice Chairman, Jim Parsons; Secretary's,
Eleanor Haase and Val Eng. Arranging Committee, Keith Willard, Mark
Ellenberger and Eleanor Haase.

page 49 and Nancy Van Den Akker followad with song on page 40. Mr.

school. Mr. McGraw led song on page 47 and 448. At this time Dr. Eskew
opened the floor for nominations for new officers. Thay are as
follows: Chairman, Dr. James Fields, Vice Chairman, Nancy Van Den Akker;

We pause at this tims each year to remember our fellow singers, listeners
and lovers of Sacred Harp music that have been called from this walk of
life in the past year Close your syes and think of one person that is no
longer with us think of a reason you can remember them so well. What is
their memory to you? Was this person your mate? Was this person your
parents? Was this person your child? Was this person a child of God?
Romans 8:16 reads: The spirit itself beareth witness with our spirit, that
we are the children of God. As we remember these peopel today, lets look
inside ourselves and think what kind of memory will I Leave? Among all
things people remember about us, we must be sure they can remember us
as children of God. Azlee Adams read the list of deceased: Hilla Delre, Va.

JAMES RIVER SACRED HARP SINGING CONVENTION
Dorey Recreation Center, Richmond, Virginia
November 21, 1992
The first annual James River Sacred Harp Convetnion was called to
order on Saturday, November 21 by Hugh HcGraw, Honorary Chairman,
leading song on page 87. The opening prayer was led by Ida Mae
Leatherman. A singing school was then conducted by Hugh mcGraw, who
focused on teaching the fundamental methods and customary practices. He
elaborated as well on the rich and abundant traditions of Sacred Harp
Singing and exhorted all to remember that Sacred Harp is a domocratic
group where singing is always done for each singer's own enjoyment. A
motion was then made and duly seconded to go into the business session.

Rudolf; Vice Chairman, Stephen McMaster; Secretary, Roberta Ciucci;
